- **[PhysCapBench: A Cross-Domain Benchmark for Physically Grounded Video Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16424)**  `arXiv:2611.16424`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Haofeng Wang, Pengcheng Wen, Ruiqi Li, Yuhao Zhang, Tiange Zhang, Boren Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Physical AI aims to learn transferable physical knowledge from real-world experience to understand, predict, and act in the physical world. Internet videos and embodied demonstrations provide complementary observations of motion, interaction, and state evolution. A foundational data challenge is to measure and preserve this physical information as training supervision. In video-language pretraining, captions express observed processes in language, yet a semantically correct description can omit the interactions and transitions that define those processes. Rich physical experience in video does not automatically translate into sufficient language supervision. This gap calls for a yardstick to assess annotation quality, compare captioning strategies, and guide training-data refinement. Motivated by our data construction for world-model pretraining, we introduce PhysCapBench, to our knowledge the first unified physical-caption benchmark spanning all three domains: third-person scenes, egocentric manipulation, and handheld-gripper demonstrations. It provides human-verified annotations for 1,500 video sequences and evaluates accuracy and coverage through seven physical dimensions. We examine downstream utility through video reconstruction and manipulation-policy fine-tuning. Physical captions reduce reconstruction LPIPS by 8.6-9.5% on third-person videos and 26.9-30.9% on egocentric videos relative to metadata captions. Training-instruction enrichment improves both PhysCapBench scores and policy success, including a 7.5-percentage-point gain for pi0.5 on a RoboTwin 2.0 subtask. PhysCapBench makes the physical content of language supervision inspectable and comparable, providing a cross-domain basis for its evaluation and improvement.
  </details>

- **[Iris-Z: Scaling One Generative Model Past Specialist Geometry Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16010)**  `arXiv:2611.16010`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Alejandro Cañada, Chema Garabito_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language moved from many task-specific models to one unified model: a single generative model with a single generative training task that handles all of them within the same backbone, and keeps improving with data, parameters and compute. We believe the bitter lesson applies just as much to computer vision, 3D and spatial understanding, in the end to visual intelligence: the effort should go into general architectures that solve all these tasks in a unified way, and into scaling them, rather than into task-specific designs. We test this on monocular geometry, a task that has belonged to specialised architectures for years. Iris-Z is a general image generator that produces appearance and geometry together. A single backbone denoises, reasons about and understands the image and its depth at the same time, and one set of weights generates images with their depth, estimates depth from photographs and paints images onto a given depth. At enough scale it surpasses state-of-the-art specialists on established depth benchmarks while keeping the generative abilities of the backbone it started from without specialized layers. We release two models for the community.
  </details>

- **[DuoJEPA: Cross-Modal Translation Between Unaligned Self-Supervised Embedding Spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15968)**  `arXiv:2611.15968`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Mahmut S. Gokmen, Mitchell A. Klusty, Evan W. Damron, Caroline N. Leach, Samuel E. Armstrong, V.K. Cody Bumgardner_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learning from paired modalities is commonly equated with aligning them: two encoders are trained until the embeddings of a pair coincide in a shared space. We study whether cross-modal retrieval and classification can instead be obtained while the two encoders' own embedding spaces remain directly unaligned. DuoJEPA trains one encoder per modality with the self-supervised LeJEPA objective and appends to each encoder a single transformer layer that translates its embedding into the other encoder's space, supervised by a contrastive objective computed entirely within that target space. No loss term compares the two own spaces directly, although the encoders are optimised jointly through the translation objective. In our experiments, the translator's residual connections determine where the cross-modal pressure is absorbed: with a residual translator the two encoders become directly aligned, whereas with a skipless (residual-free) translator a paired-cosine test detects no alignment in the final models, and at most a weak one in the ablations, at comparable cross-modal performance. The learned class-token translation is well approximated by a single scaled rotation of the source space. We evaluate DuoJEPA on two modality pairs that differ in nearly every respect. On chest radiographs and reports from CheXpert Plus, it attains a zero-shot AUC of 86.6 and a cross-modal retrieval score of 78.5, compared with 60.0 and 64.2 for our reimplementation of LeVLJEPA with the same encoders and data. On Sentinel-2 optical and Sentinel-1 radar imagery, the frozen encoders fall within the range of published Sentinel foundation models under our reproduction of the Copernicus-Bench linear-probe protocol, the translator retrieves the paired image of the other sensor among 4,000 candidates in 77-79% of cases, and a patch-level extension transfers the within-image similarity structure of the scene.
  </details>

- **[VibePrevisBench: Evaluating Agentic Coding for 4D Scene Generation in Blender](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15789)**  `arXiv:2611.15789`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuzhe Chen, Muxi Diao, Lele Yang, Wenbin Xu, Xuyang Liu, Qile Su, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Controllable real-world video generation models can condition on a rendered graybox proxy video, which fixes layout, motion and camera. Coding agents can build such videos by reconstructing a 4D scene in Blender, yet no benchmark checks both these videos and the long, multi-shot scenes behind them. We introduce VibePrevisBench, a benchmark for 4D scene reconstruction: an agent turns a brief into an executable 4D scene and renders its proxy video. Its 242 tasks span four input kinds, including scripts, photos, continuations, and reference videos. Each delivery is scored along two evaluation dimensions. Rule-based Scene Check re-runs the code and compares the exported 4D scene with hidden ground truth facts in nine dimensions. VLM-based Screen Check asks a VLM judge whether the facts that pass can be recognized in the proxy video. We analyze the evaluation results and trajectories of 11 frontier coding agents and identify three key observations. First, multimodal understanding separates the agents, especially in understanding space and camera motion. Second, all agents seem to already master basic camera work and staging, and fail at challenging requests. Third, agents inspect their own renders but fail to correct their errors. Agents thus need better visual understanding of space and camera, explicit checks for challenging requests, and item-by-item verification of renders against the brief.
  </details>

- **[MemInStream: Memorizing or Merely Recognizing? Rethinking Streaming Video Memory Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15640)**  `arXiv:2611.15640`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zhenqi He, Dunyuan Xu, Yang Bo, Kien T. Pham, Yanghao Wang, Shuhan Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Streaming video understanding aims to continuously process incoming visual observations, making the retention and retrieval of relevant information increasingly important as the video history grows. Although existing benchmarks include memory-related questions, question-answering accuracy alone does not reliably measure memory capabilities. Incorrect predictions may arise from failures in visual understanding rather than forgetting, whereas correct predictions may rely on linguistic priors or recent observations without requiring historical memory. To address these ambiguities, we introduce MemInStream, a benchmark dedicated to evaluating memory capabilities in streaming video understanding. We develop an evidence-controlled validation protocol that associates each question with temporally localized evidence intervals. The protocol validates evidence solvability and memory necessity by retaining questions that models can answer from the annotated evidence but cannot resolve using recent observations, post-evidence observations, or textual input alone. This helps disentangle memory limitations from understanding failures while reducing memory-independent shortcuts. MemInStream provides a systematic evaluation of memory capabilities through four major categories, covering Episodic Retrieval, State Tracking \& Updating, Multi-Evidence Integration, and Contextual Memory across diverse video scenarios. Overall, \textsc{MemInStream} provides a comprehensive and reliable benchmark for systematically evaluating memory capabilities in streaming video understanding.
  </details>

- **[WaveFlow-UIE: A Single-Pipeline Wavelet-Domain Flow Model for Efficient Underwater Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15457)**  `arXiv:2611.15457`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Abdulaziz Alfaraj, Mohammed Al Naser, Hassan Al Nasser, Muzammil Behzad_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater image restoration remains challenging because color distortion, scattering, and detail loss affect low- and high-frequency image components differently. Recent diffusion-based methods have shown strong restoration quality, but often rely on multi-stage pipelines and costly iterative refinement. We present WaveFlow-UIE, a single-pipeline wavelet-domain flow model that directly learns to transform degraded underwater wavelet representations into clean ones. Our method applies a discrete wavelet transform, estimates lightweight physics-inspired degradation cues including transmission and ambient light, and conditions a velocity network trained with flow matching in wavelet space. The training objective combines flow-matching, frequency-weighted reconstruction, perceptual, color-consistency, and physics-consistency losses. Across UIEB, LSUI, UFO-120, and EUVP, WaveFlow-UIE achieves a strong quality-efficiency tradeoff, with especially competitive results on perceptual metrics. Relative to a strong wavelet-based diffusion baseline, the proposed model substantially reduces inference cost while maintaining or improving restoration quality on several paired benchmarks. We show that reducing the inference process from five flow steps to a single step preserves nearly all of the restoration quality while providing an additional speedup of about $4\times$ on UIEB at $256\times256$ resolution. This single-step result suggests that a single-pass wavelet-domain model is an effective alternative to heavier diffusion-style refinement.
  </details>

- **[Reading the Unreadable: Text-Aware Image Super-Resolution Needs Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15449)**  `arXiv:2611.15449`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Jaeseong Lee, Jinwoo Kim, Jinho Jeong, Seon Joo Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-aware image super-resolution (TAISR) aims to recover high-resolution images from low-resolution inputs while preserving textual content. Existing methods treat this as a visual restoration problem, yet severely degraded text often cannot be read from local visual evidence alone. Humans instead read it by reasoning over image context, logical patterns, and prior knowledge. This raises a fundamental question: Does TAISR need reasoning? To answer this, we introduce two diagnostic tools. The first, ReasonText, is a human-curated benchmark that separates reasoning-required text from locally recoverable text. The second, Ground-Truth Text-Crop Accuracy (GTTCA), is a recognition-based metric that measures text restoration quality without text detection errors. Evaluating twelve recent super-resolution (SR) models, we find a consistent and substantial performance drop on reasoning-required text, revealing a systematic blind spot. Two observations point to a remedy. One is that text-conditioned SR models restore text substantially better when given ground-truth text. The other is that multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can read degraded text in low-resolution images through reasoning. We therefore propose Reasoning Transfer via Captioning (RTC), a training-free plug-in that uses an external MLLM to infer the degraded text and feeds the resulting caption to a text-conditioned SR model. RTC consistently improves all six text-conditioned SR models we evaluate, especially on reasoning-required text. Surprisingly, a real-world SR model with RTC can even outperform the TAISR model with RTC, suggesting that reasoning should be a core design axis for future TAISR.
  </details>

- **[Where is My Mug? Training-Free Framework for Personalized Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15216)**  `arXiv:2611.15216`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Quang Qui-Vinh Nguyen, Hung Nguyen, Rang Nguyen, Khoi Nguyen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  \textbf{Personalized Image Segmentation (PIS)} aims to segment the specific object in a target image that matches a user-provided reference. Unlike category-based segmentation or few-shot segmentation, PIS must discriminate between multiple visually similar instances, a requirement central to deployed applications such as augmented reality, personalized content editing, robotics manipulation, inventory tracking, and fine-grained visual retrieval. We show that the strong numbers reported by existing PIS methods are largely an artifact of single-instance benchmarks: when evaluated on realistic scenes with several same-category look-alikes, prior point-prompt methods collapse, because the correct instance is frequently never surfaced as a prompt. We trace this to a recall bottleneck rather than a mask-quality one, and address it with a training-free, modular system. The pipeline first generates high-recall object proposals using an off-the-shelf class-agnostic detector, then matches each proposal to the reference using foundation-model features (e.g., BLIP-2), and finally refines the selected box with SAM. For robust matching we propose \textbf{top-$K$ Sinkhorn}, a patch-level distance that scores each candidate only on its most confidently matched patches; trimming the Sinkhorn dual potentials recovers the robustness of partial optimal transport at more than $100\times$ lower cost, keeping it inside the per-proposal candidate loop. To evaluate this regime, we introduce \textbf{\datasetname}, a realistic multi-instance benchmark. Across \datasetname, PerSeg, PerMIS, and DAVIS, our system surpasses PerSAM, $\mathrm{P}^2\mathrm{SAM}$, and PDM by up to $\sim\!48$ mIoU while matching their runtime and using fewer parameters, establishing a strong, deployable training-free baseline for real-world personalized segmentation.
  </details>

- **[GingivitisSeg: A Public Dataset and Comparative Benchmark for Gingivitis Segmentation in Intraoral Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15184)**  `arXiv:2611.15184`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zeynep Ozdemir, Zehra Hasgul, Irem Ulku, Omer Ozgur Tanriover, Fatma Karacaoglu, Murat Akkaya_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Gingivitis segmentation from intraoral photographs can support computer-assisted periodontal assessment, but pixel-level delineation remains challenging due to severe foreground-background class imbalance, low-contrast boundaries, and variations in illumination and anatomy. We introduce GingivitisSeg, a public benchmark comprising 961 intraoral images with pixel-level annotations of gingival inflammation and predefined training (672), validation (144), and test (145) partitions. Seven semantic segmentation model-backbone configurations were compared using standardized partitions, input resolution, and evaluation metrics. Controlled ablation experiments examined class-weighted cross-entropy loss, Dice loss, and auxiliary supervision. UPerNet with ResNet50 achieved the highest mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) of 76.49% and gingivitis IoU of 54.91%. However, differences from DeepLabV3+ with ResNet50 and SegFormer with MiT-B2 were not statistically significant after Holm correction. The evaluated optimization components improved performance when combined in the UPerNet-ResNet50 configuration. Further analyses showed that performance was associated with gingivitis region size and decreased under stronger image blur. The dataset, benchmark splits, and source code are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Reframing Video Inverse Tone Mapping as Blind Posterior Inference via Latent Luminance Supervision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15146)**  `arXiv:2611.15146`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Hanwool Sul, Chang-Hoon Jeong, Kisung Shin, Bomseok Park, Donghoon Cha, Hansl von Kwon, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-dynamic-range (HDR) displays are now common, but most video content, such as archival footage, broadcast material, and the output of generative video models, exists only in standard dynamic range (SDR). The need is sharpest for generated video, which has no HDR master at all and is graded alongside live-action and visual-effects material finished in HDR. Converting SDR to HDR means undoing a tone-mapping operation that was never recorded, and clipping destroys the radiance wherever it acts, so HDR recovery is a blind inverse problem. Methods built on generative video priors fill clipped regions with plausible structure, but their reconstructions are systematically biased: in every public method we test, saturated content leans too bright or too dark, and the direction of the lean moves with the tone operator behind the input. We call this failure the luminance error, measure both its magnitude and its sign, and make it the focus of this work. Reframing ITM as posterior inference under an unknown operator locates the failure in two places and yields two corrections. A fixed affine functional of the video-diffusion latent reads log-luminance without decoding; on that coordinate an asymmetric loss selects an expectile of the posterior, applied only where clipping leaves luminance unconstrained. We also synthesize training SDR through an explicit family of tone operators, so the model marginalizes over what the observation cannot show. Trained this way, our luminance error stays flatter across operators and smaller on average than any public method, and the full system achieves the best perceptual quality on all three benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[What Entropy-Based View Selection Cannot See: An Audit of Test-Time Adaptation for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.14686)**  `arXiv:2611.14686`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Sivasuthan Sukumar, Uthayasanker Thayasivam_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models such as CLIP can classify images into categories they were never explicitly trained to recognize, but accuracy degrades when test images differ in style or source from their training data. A common remedy generates randomly cropped and flipped copies of each test image, called views, and combines their predictions. Since some views miss the object, methods must identify which views to trust. The dominant approach uses prediction entropy to measure confidence and retains the most certain views. Other methods use signals such as embedding typicality. Despite repeated refinements of these scoring functions, the information they rely on, the model's own response to each view, remains largely unexamined. We investigate this choice while holding other pipeline stages fixed. First, we show that view selection, rather than the gradient update, accounts for almost all accuracy gains. We then examine whether entropy identifies useful views when predictions are wrong, despite at least one view predicting the correct class. In these cases, entropy ranks correct views above incorrect ones no better than chance, recovering only about one-twentieth of the accuracy recovered by an oracle over the same views. This finding holds across two image encoders and under a control ruling out an obvious statistical artifact. An exact analysis explains this limitation: entropy measures confidence but not which class a view favors. We identify an unused signal already available in the augmentation pipeline: the fraction of the original image retained by each crop. Ranking views by this quantity improves accuracy on 14 of 15 benchmark datasets over entropy and on 11 of 15 over the strongest scoring function tested. A pre-method measurement also predicts which datasets benefit.
  </details>

- **[AVC-Reward: Event-Centric Audio-Video Consistency Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22321)**  `arXiv:2610.22321`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuruo Wu, Kaihang Pan, Kai Shen, Jiacheng Li, Shuyuan Tu, Li Tuoyu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As joint audio-video generation models become more capable, they can generate audio-visual scenes exhibiting three forms of complexity: multi-event composition, multi-layer audio mixture, and asymmetric cross-modal relations. However, current evaluations largely reuse metrics from earlier simple and unidirectional tasks. They typically evaluate an audio-video clip as a whole and rely on simple predefined relations, making them unsuitable for these increasingly complex scenes. We propose an event-centric evaluation framework that disentangles audio and visual events at the event level, analyzes each event independently, cross-validates relations across modalities, and aggregates the judgments into a coarse-to-fine, three-level consistency metric. The metric assesses global narrative harmony, event-level temporal synchronization, and fine-grained attribute alignment, providing a general formulation for audio-video consistency evaluation across diverse generation settings and cross-modal relations. We instantiate the metric as AVC-Reward, a trainable audio-video reward model. To evaluate the agreement between automatic judgments and human perception across generation paradigms and relation complexities, we construct AVC-Benchmark, which is primarily built from diverse positive and negative samples generated by various types of audio-video generation models. AVC-Reward achieves state-of-the-art alignment with human judgment among automatic methods, provides interpretable rationales that localize failures, and improves the audio-video consistency of a joint generation model when used as an online reinforcement-learning signal. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[Read, Parse, Describe: Unified Document Parsing with Visual Element Description Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20335)**  `arXiv:2610.20335`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuheng Chen, Yufan Chen, Zhuojun Cai, Ruiping Liu, Junwei Zheng, Jiale Wei, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Document understanding systems are typically evaluated as separate components via layout analysis, optical text recognition, reading-order prediction, and image captioning. This separation leaves scientific figures, captions, and visual semantics weakly connected, and fails to measure whether a model can produce a single page-level parse that is both structurally faithful and semantically accessible. We introduce Read-Parse-Describe, a unified dataset for document parsing with visual element description generation. Given a page image, models are required to output an ordered list of layout elements containing bounding boxes, semantic labels, reading-order indices, recognized textual content, and alternative text descriptions for visual elements. The benchmark is built from 2,402 documents, comprising 18,163 continuous pages, 125,218 layout elements, and 11,338 visual instances. Besides, we further propose a plug-and-play space-awareness enhancer that reuses multi-layer visual features and injects grid-aware spatial guidance into vision-language tokens for layout-aware generation. Experiments across end-to-end VLMs and pipeline parsers show that existing systems still struggle to jointly recover reading order and visual semantics, while our enhanced model achieves strong joint performance. These results highlight the need to move beyond isolated document parsing modules toward unified models that jointly read, parse, and describe complex documents. The new dataset and code will be made publicly available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[CamChoreo: Benchmarking Temporally Grounded Compositional Camera Motion Understanding for Vision Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10932)**  `arXiv:2608.10932`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Dazhao Du, Shiyan Du, Jian Liu, Yongjian Yu, Bohai Gu, Tao Han, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding camera motion is fundamental to video perception, with applications in spatial intelligence and controllable video generation. Existing camera motion evaluations typically assign labels to entire clips, overlooking within-shot changes and simultaneous movements. We therefore formulate camera motion understanding as temporally grounded, compositional recognition, requiring models to localize motion-consistent intervals and identify all active movements. We introduce CamChoreo, the first benchmark for this task, comprising 4,229 diverse single-shot clips with expert temporal annotations using 20 direction-aware labels. Nearly half of its segments contain compound camera motion. We also construct a separate training corpus of approximately 43K clips with the same annotation format. On CamChoreo, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on this corpus achieves 62.1/64.1 micro F1 with 4B/8B backbones, outperforming advanced closed-source vision language models (VLMs). To complement the primarily semantic representations of VLM visual encoders with cross-frame geometry, we introduce CamInject, which injects features from a frozen 3D foundation model into the VLM. CamInject improves over SFT but requires running the costly 3D model at inference. We instead propose CamDistill, which distills the same geometric knowledge into lightweight camera tokens during training and removes the 3D model at inference. CamDistill improves micro F1 over same-data SFT by 5.4/3.7 points on 4B/8B backbones, closely approaching CamInject. CamDistill-8B also demonstrates strong zero-shot transfer on the external camera motion understanding benchmarks CameraBench and CameraMotionVQA. Together, these resources and CamDistill enable benchmarking and improving temporally grounded, compositional camera motion understanding.
  </details>

- **[NEXUS: Neural Energy Fields for Physically Consistent Contact-Rich 3D Object Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15015)**  `arXiv:2606.15015`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Qizhen Ying, Guangming Wang, Yangchen Pan, Victor Adrian Prisacariu, Brian Sheil, Yixiong Jing_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Physics-grounded video generation requires controllable 3D object dynamics that remain physically consistent under contact, deformation, and external forcing. Existing trajectory-based methods often model isolated physical effects, making it difficult to compose conservative and non-conservative dynamics in contact-rich 3D scenes. We present NEXUS, a neural energy-field framework for contact-rich 3D object dynamics. NEXUS represents each object as a structural graph and constructs dynamic object-object and object-environment contact graphs. Inspired by Hamiltonian Neural Networks, NEXUS formulates motion through scalar energy and dissipation terms rather than directly predicting states or accelerations. Conservative effects, including gravity and elastic deformation, are composed as additive energy terms, while non-conservative effects such as damping and impact-induced energy loss are modeled with learned Rayleigh-style dissipation. Forces are derived by differentiating the energy and dissipation functions and rolled out with a multi-substep semi-implicit integrator. Across controlled trajectory benchmarks, NEXUS improves long-horizon accuracy over representative learned and physics-structured dynamics baselines under varying mechanical properties and physical-effect compositions. We further show that NEXUS trajectories provide effective guidance for contact-rich video generation, improving physical plausibility while maintaining competitive visual quality.
  </details>

- **[AgroOmni: A Large-Scale Multi-view Agricultural Dataset for Cross-Scale Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14342)**  `arXiv:2603.14342`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Jiarui Zhang, Junqi Hu, Zurong Mai, Yang Liu, Yuhang Chen, Shuohong Lou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Modern agricultural data is sourced from diverse platforms and spans multiple spatial scales, ranging from ground-level close-up photography to Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) aerial observation and satellite remote sensing imagery. Accordingly, agricultural multimodal reasoning demands robust cross-scale spatial understanding. However, due to the lack of multi-view agricultural benchmark datasets, existing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) exhibit severe ground-level bias, which leads to scale confusion then semantic collapse in agricultural perception tasks, such as misinterpreting farmland imagery as walls or floors. To address this, we introduce AgroOmni, a large-scale multi-view training corpus with 268,119 Visual Question Answering pairs covering 56 specialized task categories across 14 task types, designed to capture diverse scales in modern precisionthis http URLon this dataset, we propose AgroNVILA, which achieves a new state-of-the-art of 59.86% on the AgroMind benchmark (+12.57 over GPT-5.2), effectively mitigating the multi-view cross-scale gap for holistic agricultural understanding. Diagnostic evaluations on AgMMU further reveal an inherent heterogeneity between macro-priors and micro-diagnostics through constrained zero-shot performance. Meanwhile, even minimal fine-tuning leads to a dramatic performance gain of AgroNVILA on AgMMU, strongly demonstrating its generalization capability empowered by AgroOmni. Full training scripts are publicly available atthis https URL, and the AgroOmni dataset is released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[VMonarch: Efficient Video Diffusion Transformers with Structured Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22275)**  `arXiv:2601.22275`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Cheng Liang, Haoxian Chen, Liang Hou, Qi Fan, Gangshan Wu, Xin Tao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The quadratic complexity of the attention mechanism severely limits the context scalability of Video Diffusion Transformers (DiTs). We find that the highly sparse spatio-temporal attention patterns exhibited in Video DiTs can be naturally represented by the Monarch matrix. It is a class of structured matrices with flexible sparsity, enabling sub-quadratic attention via an alternating minimization algorithm. Accordingly, we propose VMonarch, a novel attention mechanism for Video DiTs that enables efficient computation over the dynamic sparse patterns with structured Monarch matrices. First, we adapt spatio-temporal Monarch factorization to explicitly capture the intra-frame and inter-frame correlations of the video data. Second, we introduce a recomputation strategy to mitigate artifacts arising from instabilities during alternating minimization of Monarch matrices. Third, we propose a novel online entropy algorithm fused into FlashAttention, enabling fast Monarch matrix updates for long sequences. Extensive experiments demonstrate that VMonarch achieves comparable or superior generation quality to full attention on VBench after minimal tuning. It overcomes the attention bottleneck in Video DiTs, reduces attention FLOPs by a factor of 17.5, and achieves a speedup of over 5x in attention computation for long videos, surpassing state-of-the-art sparse attention methods at 90% sparsity. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Towards Accurate UAV Image Perception: Guiding Vision-Language Models with Stronger Task Prompts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.07302)**  `arXiv:2512.07302`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Mingning Guo, Mengwei Wu, Shaoxian Li, Haifeng Li, Chao Tao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  UAV image perception with VLMs aims to identify and localize targets based on semantic and spatial task prompts. However, small, densely distributed objects and cluttered backgrounds hinder these tasks, particularly when concise prompts provide insufficient guidance. Although task-specific fine-tuning improves image-text alignment, it requires large-scale annotations and model-specific updates, increasing adaptation costs across models and tasks. To address this, we propose AerialVP, a task-adaptive, training-free agent that adapts VLMs through prompt enhancement rather than parameter optimization. AerialVP dynamically adapts its enhancement strategy to the original prompt's specificity, selectively incorporating image-derived semantics, spatial positions, and object relationships. These cues provide discriminative descriptions and spatial constraints, leveraging VLMs' pretrained alignment and reasoning capabilities to improve image-text alignment and reduce target ambiguity. Specifically, an LLM-based Task Engine coordinates modular tools for prompt analysis, visual information extraction, and prompt fusion. The enhanced prompt and original image are then provided to the unchanged backend VLM for prediction. This prompt-centric strategy enables training-free adaptation across diverse open-source and proprietary VLMs. For systematic evaluation, we introduce AerialSense, a unified benchmark covering Aerial Visual Grounding, Aerial Visual Reasoning, and Aerial Visual Question Answering across diverse UAV scenes and imaging conditions. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements across all three tasks and evaluated VLMs. AerialVP also achieves competitive performance against LoRA-based instruction fine-tuning, validating prompt-centric adaptation. Our work is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[TTB: Test-time Baking for Efficient Rendering of Decoder-only View Synthesis Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17331)**  `arXiv:2611.17331`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chin-Yang Lin, Ryo Hachiuma, Jaesung Choe, Min-Hung Chen, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang, Yen-Yu Lin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Decoder-only Large View Synthesis Models (LVSMs) with KV-cache have recently achieved state-of-the-art quality by regressing novel views from neural networks without reconstructing 3D geometry, while Test-Time Training (TTT) layers save computation but sacrifice quality. We propose Test-Time Baking (TTB), a strategy that learns to bake global scene information from cross-view attention into a lightweight MLP, enabling fast and high-fidelity novel-view decoding. The first key is to repurpose the fast-weight MLP of TTT to learn the cross-view attention mapping explicitly from its input-output pairs, rather than replacing attention entirely, achieving the same rendering efficiency as TTT while delivering substantially higher quality. The second key addresses a modality misalignment between context prefilling and novel-view rendering: we disentangle context tokens into camera-only context rendering tokens to serve as TTB input, and context ground-truth tokens with image content to serve as the TTB mapping target, ensuring the baked MLPs are relieved of the modality generalization challenge between context and novel-view processing. Our method achieves up to **23x** rendering speedup with quality on par with the state-of-the-art across several benchmarks. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[K2V: Generative Keyframing For Physically Plausible Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17324)**  `arXiv:2611.17324`  `cs.CV`  
  _Felix O'Mahony, Daniel Olmeda Reino, Roberto Cipolla, Ayush Tewari_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video diffusion models are able to generate samples with high-quality appearance and textural qualities. However, content produced by these methods is known to exhibit significant physical errors. This is because, in a classical video generation setting, all physical reasoning must take place simultaneously and exclusively within the diffusionthis http URLpropose to resolve these issues by redistributing the task of physical reasoning onto a language model-backed keyframe generation module, which allows for explicit reasoning in language, and fine-grained control over the appearance of the video at important moments in the video. We then condition a video generation model with these keyframes, reducing the role of the diffusion model to the simpler task of interpolating between physically-correct intermediate states, rather than inferring an entire sequence with diffusion. Results demonstrate that our approach significantly outperforms existing approaches, achieving state-of-the-art performance on the Physics-IQ Verified benchmark and PhyGenBench.
  </details>

- **[Consensus Capture in Reference-Based UAV Video Stabilization: A Benchmark on Dynamic Urban Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17190)**  `arXiv:2611.17190`  `cs.CV`  
  _Robert Fonod, Nikolas Geroliminis_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reference-frame stabilization registers every frame of a hovering-drone video onto one reference frame, keeping object positions comparable. The registration pipelines it relies on are selected on feature-matching benchmarks in which only the camera moves. Over a busy intersection, vehicles moving together supply mutually consistent correspondences that can draw the robust homography estimate toward their motion: consensus capture, which inlier statistics cannot flag. A two-part benchmark measures it and the object masking meant to prevent it. A static protocol scores ten pipelines under exactly known homographies on 4K aerial frames, tuned on a site-disjoint split at a matched resolution and feature budget. A dynamic protocol scores them at an offline and a real-time budget on hovers selected from the video alone to span the amount and coherence of moving foreground, plus a stationary control. It measures background alignment, object position error and the share of a moving group's displacement an estimate follows, against a pseudo-ground-truth reference audited with manually annotated ground control points, which also score every candidate. On static scenes, tuned AKAZE, RootSIFT, SIFT and BRISK lead, retuning the matcher thresholds raises the mean average accuracy (mAA) by at most 0.0062, and masking costs less than 0.005. Unmasked ORB is captured on 28 of 304 real-time dynamic pairs and on 6 offline; its real-time captured estimates have a median corner error of 18.8 px yet typical inlier counts and ratios. Masking removes capture for every pipeline and leaves the leading pipelines unchanged. Code, data and results are released.
  </details>

- **[SpotDiff: A Plug-and-Play Framework for Zero-Shot Change Detection in Remote Sensing Imagery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17172)**  `arXiv:2611.17172`  `cs.CV`  
  _Anit Upadhyaya, Ata Akbari Asanjan, Chaitanya Jalluri, Subash Yeggina, Jeremy Kravitz_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Remote sensing change detection usually relies on large collections of temporally aligned, pixel-level annotations, which limits its scalability across regions, imaging platforms and application domains. Recent zero-shot approaches reduce the need for supervision, but they often localize fine-grained changes poorly because their features are coarse or inconsistent across time. We present SpotDiff, a training-free, plug-and-play framework for zero-shot change detection built by composing complementary vision foundation models. SpotDiff uses DINOv3 for semantically robust features, AnyUp to reconstruct them at pixel resolution, and SAM 3 for text-guided region-of-interest proposals, which an inference-time multi-scale strategy (SWIMS) makes reliable on small objects. Change is then measured by comparing the reconstructed features of the two dates inside the proposed regions, with no learnable components, fine-tuning or change labels. To our knowledge, SpotDiff is the first change detection method to use a universal feature upsampler, which alone adds 13.8 F1 over bilinear upsampling of the same features. On LEVIR-CD, SpotDiff reaches 82.46 F1, 2.06 points above the strongest prior training-free method, and on the multi-class SECOND benchmark it improves building change detection by 10.3 IoU and the six-class average by 2.2 IoU. Qualitative results on PlanetScope (3 m) and Sentinel-2 (10 m) imagery show that the same pipeline transfers across resolutions without modification. Our results indicate that composing modern foundation models is an effective route to scalable, label-free change detection.
  </details>

- **[HiVE-VQA: Hierarchical Interaction via Volumetric Embedding for Video Quality Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17146)**  `arXiv:2611.17146`  `cs.CV`  
  _Robbie Hamilton, Fan Zhang, David Bull_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The proliferation of user-generated video content has made accurate and robust no-reference video quality assessment (NR-VQA) a highly important challenge. Existing research increasingly relies on architecturally complex multi-branch pipelines or computationally intensive fine-tuning of single-branch backbones. In this paper, we investigate whether a single frozen video foundation model can serve as an effective NR-VQA backbone, and propose HiVE-VQA, the first framework (to our knowledge) to apply a JEPA-based video encoder to this task. HiVE-VQA extracts token grids from multiple depths of a pre-trained V-JEPA 2.1 encoder and stacks them into a hierarchical spatio-temporal volume, which a compact 3D convolutional head fuses across its layer, spatial, and temporal axes, combining low-level distortion cues from shallow layers with the semantic and temporal context captured at greater depths. The encoder remains frozen throughout, so only the head is trained: 5.2M parameters (5.7\% of the total), with no auxiliary multi-modal models and an inference cost that is invariant to video resolution. Trained solely on LSVQ and evaluated without any fine-tuning, HiVE-VQA outperforms 17 state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods in SROCC and PLCC on both intra-dataset (LSVQ-test, LSVQ-1080p) and cross-dataset (KoNViD-1k, LIVE-VQC) benchmarks. These results indicate that hierarchical, depth-wise fusion of features from a single frozen video foundation model provides a more effective and efficient basis for NR-VQA than task-specific multi-network fusion. Code and model checkpoints are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[TokenStream: Multi-Subject Personalized Video Generation with Token-wise LoRA and Guided Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16897)**  `arXiv:2611.16897`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hung Nguyen, Quang Qui-Vinh Nguyen, Khoi Nguyen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce TokenStream, an optimization-based framework that composes independently learned subject identities in text-to-video generation without a reference-video training dataset or joint optimization of each new subject combination. Learning from a single reference image makes subject appearance difficult to separate from static-image bias, while merging independently trained adapters can mix identities. Token-wise LoRA (ToRA) restricts low-rank updates to the cross-attention value projections of subject-associated text tokens, providing a separate adaptation path for each subject. A T2V-guided stream copies queries and keys from the frozen base model during early denoising to reuse its layout and motion priors while retaining personalized values. We also introduce Multi-DB, a 30-subject, 122-prompt benchmark with appearance-free prompts and varied interactions. On Multi-DB at 1.3B, TokenStream improves identity preservation from 0.422 for DreamBooth to 0.467 and textual alignment from 0.652 to 0.688.
  </details>

- **[Visual Cipher Bench: Benchmarking LLM Agents on Long-Horizon Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16861)**  `arXiv:2611.16861`  `cs.CV`  
  _Deyao Hong, Jiayi Jiang, Yuchun Chen, Yixuan Shi, Wenyi Li, Zheng Jiang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While language-model agents excel at long-horizon coding and terminal tasks, this success relies on two conditions: explicit text specifications and immediate execution feedback. However, neither condition holds in real-world problems where critical information exists only in images, leaving it unclear whether long-horizon capability transfers from text to visual reasoning. In such visual settings, because rules and clues resist text transcription and lack test-case feedback, agents must repeatedly inspect images, simulate spatial transformations, and autonomously cross-check clues. Despite this challenge, existing evaluations fail to capture such capability: visual reasoning benchmarks largely reduce tasks to single-turn, short-chain question answering, whereas agent benchmarks focus on text-based environments or interactive states that permit trial-and-error. To bridge this gap, we introduce Visual Cipher Bench, a collection of 40 original visual puzzles across four categories: spatial manipulation and simulation, logical constraints and planning, rule induction, and knowledge-based decoding. Each puzzle provides only one to three black-and-white line drawings and a minimal submission instruction, requiring agents to discover the underlying solving mechanism entirely from the images. Across 29 reasoning-effort configurations of eight frontier models, even the strongest model, GPT-6 Astra (max), achieves only 26.7% accuracy, while five of the eight models score below 1%. Yet on a ten-task subset, providing a single non-spoiler mechanism hint boosts accuracy from 7.8% to 55.6%. This sharp contrast shows that the primary bottleneck of current agents lies not in executing multi-step plans, but in autonomously discovering rules from visual evidence. Visual Cipher Bench thus provides a rigorous benchmark for measuring long-horizon visual reasoning in agents.
  </details>

- **[Kosmo-GFM: A Production-Ready Geometry Foundation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16840)**  `arXiv:2611.16840`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sheng Tang, Shengzhe Wei, Tiancheng Chen, Wenhua Jin, Xinyang Lu, Tinghao Jiang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present Kosmo-GFM, a scene reconstruction system that integrates a geometry foundation model (GFM), a pose optimization engine (PoseEngine), and reconstruction-derived supervision from KosmoWorld. GFM predicts camera parameters, depth, cross-view correspondences, and dynamic 3D points from images and videos. PoseEngine combines these predictions with geometric optimization to recover scene-level camera poses and geometry for downstream 3D and 4D Gaussian Splatting. KosmoWorld generates images and geometric supervision from reconstructed real-world scenes to improve GFM's geometric predictions. On the Sparse and Medium subsets of SpatialBench, GFM achieves the lowest absolute relative depth error and the highest relative-pose AUC at 5 degrees among the compared methods. It also achieves the lowest mean endpoint errors for dynamic points and scene flow across the five evaluated datasets. Across 34 scenes completed by all compared reconstruction systems, PoseEngine achieves the highest scene-averaged pose AUC at thresholds of 1, 3, and 5 degrees, with a cumulative runtime of 14.07 hours, approximately 20% of GLUEMAP's 69.67 hours. PoseEngine Flash provides a further accuracy-efficiency trade-off, with a measured mixed-precision runtime of 2.62 hours, approximately 4% of GLUEMAP's runtime. Separate scaling experiments show generally favorable trends with increased data, model computation, and training budgets. These results support the use of learned geometric priors within a scene-level reconstruction pipeline.
  </details>

- **[EG360: Efficient and Generalizable $360^\circ$ Depth Estimation via Adaptive Panorama-Perspective Distillation and Spherical Cross-Level Feature Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16793)**  `arXiv:2611.16793`  `cs.CV`  
  _Laiyan Ding, Chongxi Meng, Rui Huang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generalized panorama depth estimation has witnessed great advances in recent years. These methods typically achieve robust zero-shot generalization performance using large pretrained models, but their high computational cost makes real-time inference on embedded platforms challenging. In contrast, previous lightweight panorama models have mostly been developed for single-domain evaluations and cannot generalize well due to domain shifts. To reduce the gap between these two types of models, we introduce EG360, an efficient and generalizable panorama depth estimation model. Specifically, building on a lightweight depth model, we design an Adaptive Panorama-Perspective Distillation (APPD) strategy guided by scene geometry, along with a Spherical Cross-Level Feature Fusion (SCFF) module for global semantic perception. As a result, with only 6.593M parameters after reparameterization, EG360 achieves strong zero-shot performance on real-world benchmarks and runs at 35 FPS on a Jetson Orin NX 16GB in MAXN mode, whereas a previous ViT-small model runs at less than 2 FPS. Our code will also be made public for future research.
  </details>

- **[Fog-FWL: A Large-Scale Full-Waveform LiDAR Dataset for Recovering Fog-Hidden Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16742)**  `arXiv:2611.16742`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kazuma Ikeda, Kotaro Oishi, Ryosei Hara, Ryo Yoshida, Takahiro Kado, Ibuki Fujioka, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  LiDAR is a core sensor for autonomous driving, yet it is highly vulnerable to fog. Fog scattering produces strong backscatter near the sensor and attenuates reflections from objects behind it, so weak object returns are often discarded by the peak detection that converts received signals into point clouds. Full-waveform LiDAR (FWL), which records the received optical signal before peak detection, retains these weak returns, but the lack of suitable data has prevented research on recovering fog-hidden objects from FWL. We present Fog-FWL, a dataset foundation for this task that consists of a real-world benchmark, Fog-FWL-Real, and a large-scale synthetic training set, Fog-FWL-Sim. Fog-FWL-Real contains 7,527 real foggy FWL frames from 8 scenes and 58 sequences, which is 579x larger than the only existing real foggy FWL data, together with histogram-level Object/Fog/Noise labels, 10,719 3D bounding boxes, and three difficulty levels defined by the attenuation of object reflections. Fog-FWL-Sim contains 12,000 frames generated by our physics-based simulator, Fog-FWL-Simulator, which integrates spatially heterogeneous fog density fields, time-resolved light transport including multiple scattering, and a photon-counting SPAD receiver model. The two subsets share the same sensor format and annotation protocol, enabling a strict sim-to-real evaluation in which no real waveforms are used for training. Our analysis shows that, under the hardest condition, object returns are included in the three strongest peaks for only 34.9% of object rays, underscoring the need for waveform-level recovery. Fog-FWL-Real, Fog-FWL-Sim, and the Fog-FWL-Simulator will be publicly released to facilitate research on fog-robust LiDAR perception.
  </details>

- **[What Limits Thermal Infrared Drone Detection? A Cross-Dataset Benchmark Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16724)**  `arXiv:2611.16724`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tamara R. Lenhard, Jan Kels, Tobias Koch_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Thermal infrared (IR) imaging enables drone detection with reduced dependence on ambient illumination, supporting continuous surveillance beyond the limitations of visible-spectrum sensing. However, substantial and often undocumented variability in camera systems, processing pipelines, and annotation practices across public IR drone datasets complicates the assessment of detector performance and cross-domain generalization. To examine these sources of variability and their implications for drone detection, we systematically characterize existing IR datasets and establish an artifact taxonomy reflecting their origins across the acquisition and processing pipeline, encompassing optical and sensor effects, tone mapping and image enhancement, and recording-related artifacts. Focusing on long-wave IR data, we investigate the impact of training data composition and model characteristics on YOLO-based detection performance, generalization, and condition-dependent failure modes. Our results demonstrate that detection performance is governed more strongly by training data composition than by YOLO generation or model capacity, while cross-dataset generalization exhibits pronounced asymmetries. Even the best-performing configuration fails to detect over 40\% of drones, with elevated miss rates for very small targets, cluttered urban backgrounds, and thermal crossover, highlighting the shortcomings of existing datasets.
  </details>

- **[ActWorld-JEPA: Action-Conditioned JEPA World Models Meet Latent Planning for End-to-End Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16579)**  `arXiv:2611.16579`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ziying Song, Shengkai Zhang, Caiyan Jia, Lei Yang, Lin Liu, Yuchen Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) enable future prediction in latent space without pixel reconstruction. However, latent predictive consistency alone does not establish the relative driving quality of candidate plans. We present ActWorld-JEPA, a joint-embedding formulation for candidate-based driving. Where Plan-Conditioned JEPA (PC-JEPA) uses global and step-wise trajectory intentions to guide recurrent latent prediction, then combines the predicted future with the current scene through gated cross-attention; Relational Residual Reranking ($R^3$) models agreement and discrepancy between plan and future representations under planning supervision, yielding bounded, gated residual corrections to the base planning scores. This formulation connects latent prediction to candidate selection using only observed context and proposed trajectories at inference. Benchmark-specific implementations report 94.1 PDMS on NAVSIM v1 navtest, with the final selector calibrated using aggregate NAVTEST feedback, and 91.6 and 42.7 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2 navtest and navhard, respectively. The nuScenes evaluation reports a mean $L_2$ error of 1.20 m and a predicted collision rate of 0.76% over 1--6 s. This work provides an effective path from JEPA's predictive representations to driving decisions informed by anticipated consequences.
  </details>

- **[Can an LVLM Perceive Event Boundaries Like a Human?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16452)**  `arXiv:2611.16452`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hyolim Kang, Jungsoo Lee, Yeji Song, Whie Jung, Janghoon Cho, Juntae Lee_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) reason effectively about video content, but whether they perceive its event boundaries in a manner consistent with humans remains unclear. We first investigate this through Generic Event Boundary Detection (GEBD), which identifies event boundaries naturally perceived by humans in continuous video, without relying on predefined event categories. On Kinetics-GEBD dataset, LVLMs miss many boundaries when predicting directly from frames, but effectively refine candidates proposed by an unsupervised discovery method, achieving state-of-the-art unsupervised GEBD performance. We extend this discovery-refinement approach to hour-long egocentric video, where boundary discovery requires a broader semantic scale and whole-video LVLM refinement becomes impractical. We address these challenges through coarse-to-fine temporal discovery and localized LVLM accept/reject judgments. Human evaluation shows that the LVLM's agreement with group consensus falls within the range observed among human annotators, with disagreements concentrated on low-consensus cases. Using this pipeline, we construct EgoBoundary, a benchmark for event boundary detection in hour-scale egocentric video. Representative supervised and unsupervised methods degrade substantially on EgoBoundary, highlighting the challenge of long-horizon event boundary detection.
  </details>

- **[HIFC-IQA: Train-Free Cross-Domain Image Quality Assessment via Dual-Process Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16451)**  `arXiv:2611.16451`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yu Li, Zhengran Shen, Puchao Zhou, Yukang Ding, Yachun Mi, Boyuan Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The gap between controlled synthetic distortions and complex real-world degradations introduces a critical domain shift challenge for No-Reference Image Quality Assessment. Although Unsupervised Domain Adaptation (UDA) approaches attempt to address this issue, they remain constrained by substantial retraining overhead and cumulative error propagation inherent in iterative pseudo-labeling. Inspired by the dual-process theory of human cognition for remarkable generalization ability, we propose Hierarchical Intuitive and Fuzzy Consensus (HIFC), a completely train-free Cross-Domain IQA framework. HIFC employs asymmetric perceptual decomposition to disentangle semantic content from distortion-sensitive representations. The final quality score is fused through the synergy of two complementary branches: a memory consensus branch that retrieves a stable Fuzzy Quality Centroid from a synthetic reference gallery, and an intuitive branch that captures aesthetic polarization via zero-shot vision-language alignment. Extensive experiments show that HIFC achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmarks, attaining the highest average SRCC and PLCC among competing UDA methods. Most notably, in the challenging synthetic-to-authentic cross-domain setting, HIFC achieves a substantial performance margin over competing methods, demonstrating remarkable robustness and generalization.
  </details>

- **[RICapBench: A Fine-Grained Visual Understanding Benchmark Dataset for Vision-Language Models in Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16413)**  `arXiv:2611.16413`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xue Wang, Yubin Xu, Kun Tan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Advances in vision-language models (VLMs) have improved remote sensing image analysis and supported geospatial decision-making. However, fine-grained understanding of remote sensing imagery remains challenging for VLMs. Large variations in object scale, complex geospatial relations among instances, and limited visual detail hinder precise instance localization, visible attribute identification, and spatial relation understanding, potentially leading to descriptions that lack visual grounding. Existing datasets and benchmarks insufficiently assess end-to-end instance localization and captioning without target-specific textual cues. To support this evaluation, we introduce RICapBench, a dataset and benchmark that unifies instance localization and fine-grained captioning in remote sensing. RICapBench contains 245,466 instance-level annotations and 498,005 spatial relation tuples spanning general remote sensing, UAV remote sensing, and dense small-object scenarios. Each annotation pairs an object's oriented bounding box with a natural language description of its visible attributes and spatial relations to surrounding instances and environmental elements. To evaluate the accuracy and completeness of spatial relation captioning, we propose a remote sensing spatial relation parser and the RelCap-F1 metric. We combine RelCap-F1 with instance localization and attribute captioning metrics into a composite metric, RICScore. Our evaluation of 15 advanced open-source and closed-source VLMs identifies instance localization as a major bottleneck for these models. LocateAnything achieves the highest RICScore of 31.68, but scores lower on spatial relation captioning than other task-fine-tuned open-source VLMs. Code and dataset are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Teach with Blinks: Breaking Linguistic Momentum in On-Policy Distillation for Fine-Grained Multimodal Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16402)**  `arXiv:2611.16402`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tangzheng Lian, Hang Zhang, Guanyu Hu, Renjiu Hu, Zhensong Zhang, Jiankang Deng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  On-policy distillation (OPD) has emerged as an effective post-training approach to improving fine-grained perception in multimodal large language models by amortizing costly inference-time zooming operations into a single-pass recipe. However, vanilla OPD is inherently susceptible to linguistic momentum: the teacher can follow the student's erroneous prefixes, thus weakening corrective supervision. To address this, we propose Blink On-Policy Distillation (BlinkOPD), in which the teacher "blinks" at every token: it reads the same prefix once with its eyes open and once with its eyes closed. A first-glance gain further strengthens the correction on rollouts that conflict with the teacher. By extracting more effective supervision from each on-policy trajectory, BlinkOPD achieves state-of-the-art average performance across six fine-grained benchmarks using only 500 training images (12x less data) than existing OPD methods. Beyond fine-grained perception, BlinkOPD also demonstrates strong zero-shot improvements across ten held-out benchmarks spanning general visual perception, visual hallucination, and multimodal reasoning abilities. Code:this https URL
  </details>

- **[FuGIBench: Evaluating Reliable Preparation in Streaming Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16400)**  `arXiv:2611.16400`  `cs.CV`  
  _Changheng Cai, Zhichao Huang, Song Ren, Zhichen Zhao, Hongsen Liu, Chaoyang Zheng, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  An assistant watching a worker can prepare for an emerging need before the worker is ready to receivethis http URLthe need evolves, the assistant must maintain preparation until a response is due, or withdraw it if the need disappears. Video anticipation and interactive response benchmarks leave this interval between early evidence and response largely unmeasured. We introduce FuGIBench (Future-Grounded Interaction), a streaming-video benchmark in which a model chooses among waiting, reversible preparation, and response given a causal video prefix and a task contract. The benchmark contains 19,893 synthetic tool-assistance videos across ten tool tasks, in handover, ordinary-activity, and cancelled-need tracks. One primary score credits sustained preparation, incorporates response-interval alignment, and penalizes activity when the reference prescribes waiting. On a frozen set of 1,000 episodes, a reference system with a longer preparation lead has poorer response alignment, while similar handover leads accompany different levels of activity during ordinary work in matched scenarios. Together with cancellation analyses, these results show why evaluating preparation requires tracking decisions as needs develop, become actionable, or disappear.
  </details>

- **[Hand in Silhouette: Synthetic-to-Real Multi-View Hand Reconstruction via Binary Masks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16339)**  `arXiv:2611.16339`  `cs.CV`  
  _Leiyao Cui, Lecheng Ruan, Zihang Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Calibrated multi-view hand reconstruction has advanced rapidly, yet the effectiveness of existing methods depends strongly on real captured data, which is costly to acquire, limited in hand-geometry diversity, and often affected by imperfect annotations. Synthetic data offers a scalable alternative with accurate supervision, but a substantial synthetic-to-real appearance gap hinders its use. To address this, we introduce Hand in Silhouette (SilHand), a calibrated multi-view hand reconstruction framework that operates on binary hand masks, obtainable at low cost either automatically or manually, to reduce the synthetic-to-real observation gap. Rather than relying on RGB features for 3D lifting, SilHand projects metric 3D hypotheses into each view to query and fuse mask-ray evidence for hand reconstruction. This mask-centric formulation enables training on a large-scale synthetic dataset comprising 46.8k grasping poses and 60k free-hand samples, providing broad coverage of hand geometry while directly transferring to real captured hand geometry. Across five real-world benchmarks, SilHand improves relative reconstruction accuracy from 49.2% to 63.4% using masks rendered from reference hands. Moreover, reconstruction accuracy improves consistently as the synthetic training set grows, indicating that broader geometric coverage translates into better real-world performance. While achieving such diversity is difficult with real captured data, it becomes practical under our mask-centric formulation. Finally, SilHand remains robust under mask corruption, with reconstruction error increasing by at most 1.63 mm. Together, these results support binary masks as an effective interface for learning hand reconstruction from synthetic geometry.
  </details>

- **[DEDCA: Test-Time Adaptation for Generalized AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16307)**  `arXiv:2611.16307`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Tan, Qimin Zhang, Lihuang Fang, Kebing Jin, Jinghui Qin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  AI-generated image detectors face significant challenges when deployed in real-world environments, particularly when test samples are produced by unseen generators that deviate from the source training distribution. Existing detectors often rely on a single type of evidence, such as semantic representations or low-level generation traces, making them vulnerable when the corresponding cue becomes unreliable. Moreover, adapting detectors to newly emerging generators usually requires target labels or retraining, which is costly and impractical. To address these challenges, we propose Dual-Evidence Disagreement-Constrained Adaptation (DEDCA), a test-time adaptation framework for generalized AI-generated image detection. DEDCA constructs a dual-evidence detector by combining CLIP-based semantic evidence with trace evidence extracted from shuffled entropy and residual statistics, and introduces a reliability-aware gate to perform sample-specific adaptive fusion. Our key idea is to exploit the disagreement between the two evidence streams as an unlabeled adaptation signal, enabling the detector to adjust to target-domain shifts without relying on target labels or blindly trusting a single prediction stream. Experiments show that DEDCA achieves superior performance on the GenImage benchmark by outperforming state-of-the-art AI-generated image detectors and test-time adaptation methods with 92.0\% average accuracy.
  </details>

- **[WorldDraft: If Code Builds the World, Can Video Models Just Render It?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16217)**  `arXiv:2611.16217`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhiyu Hou, Wen-Jie Shu, Harold Haodong Chen, Weilin Ruan, Chenfei Liao, Liuzhou Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal coding agents decouple 4D world construction from visual generation, repurposing video models from world generators toward generative renderers. This division of labor raises a fundamental question: if code builds the world, can video models just render it? A reliable generative renderer hinges on two complementary criteria: (i) world preservation, retaining prescribed 4D kinematics and camera paths; and (ii) visual realization, persistently binding text-specified target attributes over time. To evaluate this capability, we introduce WorldDraft, a benchmark of $432$ strictly paired test cases across $48$ executable 4D world families spanning six dynamic challenge categories. Under a $3 \times 3$ factorial design, WorldDraft orthogonally crosses draft granularity (abstract proxy to previs) and appearance alignment (neutral to conflicting) under identical dynamics. Extensive evaluations across $12$ representative models reveal that: (I) world preservation and visual realization remain in stark operational tension; (II) richer draft scaffolding anchors structure but paradoxically impairs appearance fidelity; and (III) conflicting source cues trigger severe source leakage (up to $64\%$), exposing fragile cross-modal disentanglement. To mitigate these bottlenecks, we propose WorldDraft-Primer, a plug-and-play visual prompting strategy that consistently improves both objectives across frozen video renderers. Together, WorldDraft and WorldDraft-Primer lay a foundation for bridging programmatic 4D simulation with video generation, advancing physically grounded generative rendering.
  </details>

- **[GOOSELoc: A Multimodal Benchmark for Ground-to-Aerial Localization in Unstructured Off-Road Terrain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16162)**  `arXiv:2611.16162`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fabian Deuser, Peter Mortimer, Robin-Nico Kampa, Anna Bößendörfer, Mirko Mählisch, Martin Werner, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cross-view pose estimation estimates a ground camera's geographic pose by matching onboard sensor data with geo-referenced overhead imagery. This is particularly relevant when GNSS is unreliable, for example under forest canopy or in urban canyons. Existing metric cross-view benchmarks, such as VIGOR and cross-view KITTI, are dominated by urban and road scenes with strong human-made structure without off-road terrain. We introduce GOOSELoc, a multimodal benchmark for ground-to-aerial localization in unstructured environments, including forests, fields, and rural tracks. GOOSELoc comprises 9,356 scenes recorded across all four seasons around Munich, Germany, pairing geo-referenced aerial orthophotos with surround-view RGB imagery and 3D LiDAR point clouds. Paved/unpaved labels enable analyzing the impact of terrain structure, while the seasonal recordings allow studying the appearance gap to mostly summer-captured orthophotos. To assess the transferability of existing methods, we adapt and fine-tune four state-of-the-art cross-view localization baselines on GOOSELoc. Despite task-specific fine-tuning, the performance degrades substantially in off-road environments. We therefore propose a multimodal baseline combining surround-view imagery and LiDAR geometry, improving localization performance and providing a stronger reference for future work. Data and baselines are released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Topology-Aware 4D Gaussian Splatting for Egocentric Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16012)**  `arXiv:2611.16012`  `cs.CV`  
  _Leonardo Vanni, Stefano Gasperini, Federico Tombari, Chiara Plizzari_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing dynamic scenes and objects from monocular egocentric video is crucial for embodied AI, yet existing 4D Gaussian Splatting (4DGS) approaches struggle with everyday interactions involving topological changes, such as cutting, assembly, opening, or closing. Since 4DGS methods rely on deforming a fixed representation, events that split, merge, or reveal surfaces cause severe visual artifacts. To address this challenge, we introduce TopoEgo, the first benchmark dedicated to topological changes in egocentric video (covering fission, fusion, open, and close), alongside Topo-4DGS, a topology-aware reconstruction method. Topo4DGS jointly models part-level motion and time-varying surface support by assigning independent rigid transformations to interacting parts and dynamically activating or deactivating Gaussians as surfaces appear, disappear, or separate. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Topo-4DGS significantly outperforms state-of-the-art 4DGS approaches across topological changes, especially on post-event frames. Code and data will be made publicly available.
  </details>

- **[Image Deblending with Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15760)**  `arXiv:2611.15760`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dan Botchan, Ohad Fried, Yacov Hel-Or_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image deblending is the task of decomposing a composite image back into its source images, which is an ill-posed problem, as a single blended image can be decomposed into multiple plausible source layers. While diffusion models are often used to solve inverse problems, they typically address single-output reconstruction problems. In this work, we formulate deblending as a joint inverse problem, employing diffusion posterior sampling to simultaneously reconstruct two source images, demonstrating that a single diffusion prior is expressive enough to disentangle two full images at once rather than just one. Drawing inspiration from human perception, where semantic understanding helps disentangle visual inconsistencies, we propose a training-free framework that leverages text prompts to constrain this solution space. We address the blind deblending task through a two-stage architecture: text-guided latent diffusion performs semantic separation using prompts as high-level cues, followed by a pixel-space refinement to recover fine details. Stochastic sampling produces multiple plausible decompositions that can be ranked without ground truth. Extensive experiments show that our training-free method achieves state-of-the-art results on unseen domains and extends to spatially-varying alpha maps, while remaining competitive with supervised baselines on standard scalar blending benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[How Far Can Frozen Vision-Language Models Go on Event Data? A Training-Free Study on EventBench](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15756)**  `arXiv:2611.15756`  `cs.CV`  
  _Md Kamrujjaman Mobin, Md Salman Shamil, Md Adnan Arefeen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present a training-free system for the EBMV @ ECCV 2026 challenge "Event Stream Understanding with EventBench" (General Understanding track), which evaluates vision-language models on five event-camera understanding tasks. We use none of the challenge's training data: our goal is to measure how far off-the-shelf, RGB-pretrained models can go on event data through representation and prompting alone. For four of the five categories (gesture recognition, detailed understanding, causal reasoning, and event OCR) we render event bins as red/ON, blue/OFF frames, select a category-specific subset of bins, stitch them into a single grid image, and query the same frozen Qwen3.5 9B vision-language model with a category-specific prompt for all four tasks. The fifth category, action recognition, feeds every bin directly into the organizers' EventGPT-Plus-2B. The system reaches 62.28% overall accuracy across 2,084 test questions (rank 9/27), led by action recognition and causal reasoning (80.27%, 77.22%) and limited most by gesture recognition and event OCR (54.70%, 34.57%). We visualize test samples from every category together with the exact images the model receives, score six alternative representations against the evaluation server, and analyze the full test set post hoc. That analysis shows that our bounding-box crop almost never tightened around content, so event OCR text reached the model at roughly one fifth of its horizontal resolution, and that detailed-understanding predictions are skewed toward the first two answer options.
  </details>

- **[WaveletLoRA: Frequency-Aware Content-Style Decomposition for Personalized Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15617)**  `arXiv:2611.15617`  `cs.CV`  
  _Peiyao Wang, Jiahui Sun, Weining Wang, Jing Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Content-style decomposition from a single image is a fundamental challenge in personalized image generation, aiming to separate subject identity from visual style for flexible recomposition. Existing approaches primarily operate in the spatial domain, without explicitly exploiting the distinct frequency characteristics naturally associated with content and style. In this paper, we propose WaveletLoRA, a novel frequency-aware framework for explicit content-style decomposition through wavelet-domain supervision. Specifically, we first apply a multi-level discrete wavelet transform (DWT) to decompose the reference image into low-frequency and high-frequency subbands. We then assign a dedicated LoRA branch to each subband, where the low-frequency branch models global appearance for style acquisition, while the high-frequency branches capture structural details for content preservation. To further improve disentanglement quality, we introduce a dense Mixture-of-Experts aggregation module together with a frequency-domain regularization objective, which reduces style leakage and enhance content fidelity. In addition, we establish WaveBench and develop a VLM-based evaluation protocol that explicitly measures both attribute similarity and attribute separation for disentanglement assessment. Extensive experiments demonstrate that WaveletLoRA consistently outperforms existing methods and produces results that better align with human preference.
  </details>

- **[Cat Royale Comprehensive Dataset: A Dataset for Pose Detection, Segmentation, Animal Action Recognition and Scene Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15601)**  `arXiv:2611.15601`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zane K. J. Hartley, Joseph Ho, Alan Chamberlain, Michael P. Pound, Steve Benford_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Animal focused computer vision datasets are of a growing interest in the fields of both Computer Vision and Animal behaviour. The ability to make sense of complex scenes via a variety of Computer Vision tasks has substantial implications for behavioural understanding and welfare. The perfection of these tasks will help lead to better health and emotional outcomes for wild and domestic animals. In this work we present the Cat Royale Comprehensive Dataset (CRCD), a large, comprehensive, multi-modal dataset for feline understanding, based on a huge database of over 500 hours of video captured during the Cat Royale artwork and research project. Our dataset contains annotations for a range of computer vision problems including 20,000 images with instance segmentation masks, 5000 images with multi-subject pose coordinates, over 800 video clips with action classes, and over 800 clips with detailed text description. This is the first such dataset to link this wide range of annotation types together for multi-modal use. We release this entire dataset, and our custom annotation tools will also be made available, in order to allow for ongoing expansion. We also provide a series of benchmarks for the tasks of Instance Segmentation, Pose detection, Temporal Action Localisation, and finally video-text retrieval. The totality of this multi-modal dataset is the data needed to solve both these individual computer vision tasks as well as enabling more complex multi-modal tasks making use of a combination of annotations.
  </details>

- **[RipBench: A Unified Benchmark for Multi-Level Rip Current Detection, Classification and Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15574)**  `arXiv:2611.15574`  `cs.CV`
